data: {"type":"response.output_item.done","custom_outputs":null,"item":{"type":"function_call_output","call_id":"toolu_bdrk_01JvL73i5mDnMieMTUbxbtyA","output":"  1  ---\n  2  name: report-builder\n  3  description: Builds a self-contained HTML report from a compact JSON ReportConfig via the build_report tool. Every derived figure is computed by the engine from the evidence, so the report and the evidence cannot disagree. Use when the user asks for a report, a báo cáo, an executive summary as a document, or a shareable link to analysis results.\n  4  ---\n  5  \n  6  # Report Builder\n  7  \n  8  You write a **JSON `ReportConfig`** with two keys — `meta` and `sections` — and\n  9  `build_report` renders it into one self-contained HTML file (sidebar, charts,\n 10  sortable tables, RAG colour, light/dark, print). The template owns all markup,\n 11  styling and colour. Every block is complete where you write it, and the only\n 12  names in it are the evidence's own.\n 13  \n 14  ## The one rule about figures\n 15  \n 16  **A figure you can read off a row, you type. A figure you would have to work\n 17  out, you never type — you hand over a `calc`.** A total, a variance or a share\n 18  typed by hand is a number no evidence supports and nothing downstream can check:\n 19  \n 20  ```json\n 21  { \"label\": \"TOI YTD\", \"value\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"actual\", \"fmt\": \"ty1\" } }\n 22  ```\n 23  \n 24  not `\"value\": \"10,973.4\"`. The engine sums the same rows the table below shows,\n 25  so headline and components are two views of one sum. Every value slot takes a\n 26  calc: KPI value, `delta`, progress `ratio`, table cell, table `total`.\n 27  \n 28  **Prose is not an exception.** An `insight` carries its own `vars` — the same\n 29  calc objects — and names them in its `html` as `{name}`; the engine derives,\n 30  formats and drops the figure into the sentence at build time:\n 31  \n 32  ```json\n 33  { \"type\": \"insight\",\n 34    \"src\": { \"ev\": \"wi_bank_9f2b\", \"where\": { \"section\": \"ytd\" } },\n 35    \"vars\": { \"toi\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"actual\", \"fmt\": \"ty1\" },\n 36              \"gap\": { \"expr\": \"a - b\", \"vars\": { \"a\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"actual\" },\n 37                                                  \"b\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"budget\" } },\n 38                       \"fmt\": \"signed_ty1\" } },\n 39    \"html\": \"TOI đạt <strong>{toi} tỷ</strong>, {gap} tỷ so với kế hoạch.\" }\n 40  ```\n 41  \n 42  A `{name}` the block does not declare is a build error. A figure typed into a\n 43  sentence has not been through the engine: one replayed turn typed 113 of them.\n 44  An action `body` has no `vars`; derived figures there go in an `insight` beside it.\n 45  \n 46  ## The contract\n 47  \n 48  Every object below is closed: a key not on this card is rejected, and the error\n 49  prints the allowed list for that exact path. This card is the whole contract;\n 50  `contracts/report-config.schema.json` says the same thing in 24 KB.\n 51  \n 52  Read it as: `key*` required · after `|` optional · `∈` the complete set of\n 53  allowed values · `⊕` two exclusive shapes · `→` the key that tells a union's\n 54  members apart.\n 55  \n 56  ```text\n 57  config                meta* sections*\n 58  \n 59  meta                  title* | doc_title label meta_line brand footer lang\n 60  section               id* title* blocks* | label in_nav nav_level ∈ 1 2\n 61  \n 62  src                   ev* | where\n 63  calc  ⊕                   agg* col* | ev where fmt   (col optional: count)\n 64                            expr* vars* | fmt\n 65                            agg ∈ sum avg min max count first last\n 66  value                     a typed string or number  ⊕  a calc\n 67  fmt                   int ty0 ty1 ty2 pct0 pct1 pct2 signed_ty0 signed_ty1 signed_int usd_mn kh raw\n 68  \n 69  cf                    thresholds* | target else_class\n 70    thresholds[]        op* value* class* | value2 (required: between)\n 71                            op ∈ lt lte gt gte eq between\n 72                            class, else_class ∈ good warn bad up down neutral\n 73  \n 74  blocks[] → type\n 75    insight             type* html* | src vars   (vars: name → calc · html: {name})\n 76    subtitle            type* text* | margin_top\n 77    divider             type*\n 78    kpi_group           type* cards* | src columns ∈ 2 3\n 79      cards[]           label* value* | unit sub cf delta progress\n 80        delta           value* | suffix dir ∈ positive negative neutral auto\n 81        progress        ratio* | cap state ∈ good warn bad · rule\n 82    driver_row          type* boxes* | src\n 83      boxes[]           val* lbl* | delta\n 84    table               type* columns* | src rows total sortable variant ∈ default product\n 85      columns[]         label* | col align ∈ left right center · fmt cf\n 86      rows[][]          v* | bold align tag ∈ up down warn neutral · cf   ⊕   a value\n 87      total             cells*\n 88    chart               type* id* spec* | title sub size ∈ sm lg\n 89    chart_pair          type* charts*\n 90    actions             type* cards*\n 91      cards[]           priority* title* body* | action_id\n 92                            priority ∈ high medium\n 93        body[]          label* | html items signal\n 94          signal        text* | label\n 95  \n 96  spec → kind\n 97    bar_group | line    kind* src* x* series* | x_rotate y_name value_suffix legend\n 98      series[]          col* | name agg where type ∈ bar line · color label\n 99                            line_style ∈ solid dashed dotted\n100    pie                 kind* src* name_col* value_col* | radius value_suffix legend\n101    afb                 kind* src* month_col* actual* budget* | forecast forecast_band y_name\n102  ```\n103  \n104  Each of these has cost a whole build, or built a wrong figure:\n105  \n106  **Two unions, two different discriminators.** A **block** says `type`; a **chart\n107  spec** says `kind`. Writing `kind` on a block fails at every block at once.\n108  \n109  **A name that fits one level up is still a rejected key.** `title` belongs to a\n110  section, a chart block and an action card, and to nothing else — a `kpi_group`\n111  and a `table` have no title. `text` is `subtitle`'s; `insight` takes `html`.\n112  `items` is an action body's; `kpi_group` and `actions` take `cards`.\n113  \n114  **`x`, `col`, `name_col`, `value_col`, `month_col`, `actual`, `forecast`,\n115  `budget` are all evidence column names** — plain strings, taken from the header\n116  of the evidence that block's `src` names.\n117  \n118  **`fmt` is display only and never rescales.** `pct1` puts a sign after the\n119  number it is given: `3.9` prints `3.9%`, `0.039` prints `0.0%`. A count is `int`,\n120  a count's delta `signed_int`. **`delta.dir` is the colour**, not the direction\n121  that is good: `auto` colours by sign; name one only to override the sign.\n122  \n123  **A ratio column can arrive as a fraction** (`0.039`, `1.005`) while the\n124  sub-agent's answer says `NIM 3.85%`, and nothing but the digest tells you which\n125  you have. Put the scale in the calc, never in a typed number:\n126  `{ \"expr\": \"v * 100\", \"vars\": { \"v\": { \"agg\": \"first\", \"col\": \"nim_pct\" } }, \"fmt\": \"pct1\" }`,\n127  build, and read that line of the digest against the sub-agent's figure — `3.9%`\n128  keeps the `* 100`, `390.0%` drops it. The `* 100` in an `expr` is arithmetic on\n129  the record (the digest prints it); the rescale Data Integrity forbids is the\n130  silent one — a typed `3.9` where the evidence says `0.039`. A `src` table column\n131  has no calc, so a fraction column is shown as a `total` cell or an `insight` var,\n132  not as a column.\n133  \n134  **`sum` is for flows; a balance is read, not added.** TOI, NII, NFI and fees are\n135  a month's worth each, so a period's figure is the months added up. A balance\n136  (`eop_*`, `*_bal`, AUM) is the amount *as at* a month, and month-end balances\n137  added together are the same money counted once per month. Pin the last month in\n138  `where` and read it with `last`:\n139  `{ \"agg\": \"last\", \"col\": \"eop_bal\", \"where\": { \"rptmonth\": \"202608\" }, \"fmt\": \"ty1\" }`.\n140  `sum` folds a balance across segments at one month, never across months.\n141  \n142  The smallest config that builds:\n143  \n144  ```json\n145  { \"meta\": { \"label\": \"TECHCOMBANK · CIBG\", \"title\": \"CIBG — TOI 5T2026\",\n146              \"meta_line\": \"5T2026 · nguồn: Genie · BF Hub\" },\n147    \"sections\": [\n148      { \"id\": \"overview\", \"label\": \"01 — Tổng quan\", \"title\": \"Tổng quan CIBG\",\n149        \"blocks\": [\n150          { \"type\": \"kpi_group\",\n151            \"src\": { \"ev\": \"wi_cibg_a4c8c2c1-1\", \"where\": { \"section\": \"sector_ytd\" } },\n152            \"cards\": [ { \"label\": \"TOI YTD\", \"unit\": \"tỷ VND\",\n153                         \"value\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"col\": \"actual\", \"fmt\": \"ty1\" } } ] } ] } ] }\n154  ```\n155  \n156  ## Evidence, and the rows a block sees\n157  \n158  `src` is `{ \"ev\": \"wi_…\", \"where\": {…} }` and it is the **whole** of what a block\n159  can see. `ev` is the id inside `<evidence id=\"…\">` — not a file path;\n160  `/evidence/wi_x/data.md` is rejected. The engine takes exactly the rows you\n161  describe, so a table of any size costs you these three lines.\n162  \n163  `where` keeps the rows whose column matches: `{\"section\": \"sector_ytd\"}`, or\n164  `{\"sector\": [\"FI\", \"MMU\"]}` for any-of, compared against the value **as the\n165  evidence prints it** — `202601`, not `202601.0`; `FI`, not `Financial Institutions`.\n166  \n167  **A calc inside a block inherits that block's `src`.** Drop `ev` and `where` and\n168  it reads the block's own rows, which is why a total row repeats nothing; a\n169  `where` of its own narrows those rows, and wins on a column both name. A calc\n170  that names a *different* `ev` has left the block: the block's `where` does not\n171  travel with it. Naming the block's own `ev` again changes nothing — it is the\n172  same rows.\n173  \n174  **One block reads one evidence. There are no joins.** Two evidences meet only\n175  inside an `expr`, as numbers already reduced:\n176  \n177  ```json\n178  { \"expr\": \"cibg / total * 100\",\n179    \"vars\": { \"cibg\":  { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"ev\": \"wi_cibg_a4c8c2c1-1\", \"col\": \"actual\" },\n180              \"total\": { \"agg\": \"sum\", \"ev\": \"wi_bank_9f2b-2\",   \"col\": \"actual\" } } }\n181  ```\n182  \n183  `vars` names are local to that one object — two blocks deriving the same figure\n184  write the same calc twice and still agree, because both read the same rows.\n185  \n186  ## Blocks worth a note\n187  \n188  - **`table` takes rows from exactly one place.** `src` generates one row per row\n189    of the evidence and every column carries `col` — prefer this for anything the\n190    data already holds. `rows` spells them out, for a table that is not a view of\n191    one evidence. Declaring both is an error, and so is declaring neither. There\n192    is no pivot: one row per `data_type` in the evidence is one row per\n193    `data_type` in the table. Actual beside budget for one entity is a\n194    `kpi_group` or `driver_row` whose calcs carry their own `where`.\n195  - **`total` is what a reader checks the headline against.** Its numeric cells are\n196    calcs over the same evidence; leave `ev` and `where` out and the block's apply.\n197  - **`chart_pair` takes exactly two charts**, each with `size` `sm`.\n198  - **`afb`** draws actual, forecast and budget against one period column: give it\n199    `month_col` plus the column names. `forecast_band` shades the forecast tail.\n200  - **`actions`**: write the recommendation as content for the reader — the\n201    sentences you want printed. `action_id` is optional; the builder stamps one.\n202  - **`cf`** colours a value by threshold. Thresholds are tried in order and the\n203    first hit wins, so write them narrowest-first; `else_class` catches the rest.\n204  \n205  ## Collecting the data\n206  \n207  For a unit-level report (CBC, HSO, branch, team) ask for product × month\n208  granularity in one query, balances and budget per product included, so the\n209  report can break it down; when Genie returns aggregates only, retry once asking\n210  for product level. Carry the sub-agents' abbreviated product keys into the\n211  queries and map them to display names only in the config. An inline chart token\n212  belongs in the chat response; the report draws its own charts from `spec`.\n213  \n214  ## Workflow\n215  \n216  1. **Read a preset in `assets/examples/` fully** — it is your template, not an\n217     illustration. `performance.json` is a segment P&L: one evidence, every block\n218     type, `delta` · `progress` · `total` · RAG throughout. Start there.\n219  2. **Plan with `write_todos`, one item per section.** Decide a section's blocks\n220     when you reach it, not before.\n221  3. **Write `meta` and the first section only** to `/report_config.json` with\n222     `write_file` — the smallest config that builds, above, with your names in\n223     it — then call `build_report(config_path=\"/report_config.json\",\n224     filename=…)`. A seven-section config is ~20k tokens; typed once while\n225     reasoning and once in the call it is 40k of a 64k output ceiling, and a\n226     call that reaches the ceiling is cut with nothing saved. One section is a\n227     call the ceiling cannot reach. Then **append one section per `edit_file`**\n228     — `old_string` is the file's last two lines, `  ]` and `}` — and build\n229     again; when an edit removes a key or a column, take its comma too. A report\n230     of one or two sections is a single write.\n231  4. **Read the digest that comes back with the link** — one line per card,\n232     table and chart, by config path, showing the figure as the page prints it.\n233     A unit that contradicts its label, a `—` where a number belongs, a\n234     percentage under 1, a value 1000x off, a balance that dwarfs its own months:\n235     `edit_file` that path, build again. The digest is how you see your `fmt`\n236     and `agg` choices; nothing before it can.\n237  \n238  ## Changing a report you already built\n239  \n240  A title, a dropped chart, a reordered section, another period or comparison:\n241  `edit_file` the config you wrote, changing only the keys the request names, and\n242  build again with the same `config_path`. Every figure the user did not ask to\n243  touch comes back as it was, because each one is derived from the rows rather\n244  than carried across by hand.","duration_ms":3835,"run_ms":7,"gen_ms":3811}}

data: {"type":"response.output_item.done","custom_outputs":null,"item":{"type":"function_call_output","call_id":"toolu_bdrk_01LegsEFgqFd45FjYWS331wn","output":" 1  ---\n 2  name: action-recommendation\n 3  description: >-\n 4    Generate concrete, prioritized action recommendations from financial insights.\n 5    Read it when the user asks to go deeper — root cause, \"tại sao\", \"đề xuất\",\n 6    \"recommend\", \"action\" — or when a report is being built, since a report\n 7    carries actions; a default-depth answer leaves actions out until asked.\n 8    The researcher's brief supplies the context (Techcombank plans and campaigns\n 9    in scope, competitor moves, market signals); banking expertise turns it into\n10    3–5 actions, each moving the underperforming metric for the exact segment\n11    and product being analysed.\n12  ---\n13  \n14  ## Core principle: direct causality\n15  \n16  An action moves the metric gap within one logical step — not \"if X then Y then Z\". The test is the CEO's question, \"how does this fix the problem we just discussed?\": the answer is one sentence, or the action is out.\n17  \n18  ## Workflow\n19  \n20  1. **Identify gaps** — which metrics underperform, by how much, in which segments. A gap is a metric, a size and a direction from the evidence: `CASA HH −200 tỷ vs budget, Q3 2026`.\n21  2. **Lock scope** — segment, product, geography, period. Actions stay inside this boundary, and the brief is requested for it.\n22  3. **Brief the researcher** — `task(researcher_agent)` with the question, the scope, every gap from step 1 and a `Needs:` line; for an action turn that is `plans, campaigns, decisions, signals, competitors`. One request for this step — a brief that opened the turn answered a different question — and no grep of `/wiki/` for what it covers. The brief comes back tagged and cited — `[plan]`/`[campaign]` figures are targets, `[signal]`/`[competitor]` figures are the market's — and its `## Assessment` reads each gap as `internal`, `market`, `both` or `unresolved`.\n23  4. **Relevance Test** — `read_file` → `references/relevance-test.md`. Every candidate action, and every brief line it leans on, passes the three gates.\n24  5. **Synthesize** — brief + banking expertise → 3–5 actions. The brief's reading of a gap chooses the lever: `internal` → a TCB plan or campaign is under-delivering, the action fixes execution; `market` → a competitor or a rate moved, the action answers it; `both` → both, and the Why says which part is which. A gap the brief lists under `Not found` gets an action from banking expertise, and the Why says so.\n25  6. **Prioritise** — `read_file` → `references/evaluation-criteria.md`. Impact, Feasibility and Directness, read qualitatively.\n26  7. **Format** — per the template below.\n27  \n28  ## References (read on demand)\n29  \n30  | File | When | Content |\n31  | --- | --- | --- |\n32  | `references/relevance-test.md` | Step 4, before including any action | Three gates, causal chain, reading the brief |\n33  | `references/evaluation-criteria.md` | Step 6, after generating candidates | Priority table, hard rule, self-check, output checklist |\n34  | `references/anti-patterns.md` | When unsure an action belongs | Four anti-patterns with wrong/right pairs |\n35  \n36  ## Action format\n37  \n38  ```\n39  **🔴/🟡/🟢 [Priority] Action title**\n40  - Why: <gap> — <driver: internal / market / both> — <evidence: the analysis figure and the brief line>\n41  - Action: 1–3 concrete steps\n42  - Impact: quantified move on the metric (direct path only)\n43  - Owner: team or role\n44  ```\n45  \n46  The Why is where the brief shows: the `[plan]` target a campaign is behind on, a `[signal]` with its date and direction, a `[competitor]` move with TCB's exposure — each cited as the brief cites it, `path:line` or URL. When the brief found nothing for the gap, the Why opens with \"no TCB plan found for this gap\", so the reader knows the lever is proposed, not existing.\n47  \n48  ## Priority levels\n49  \n50  - 🔴 **High** — closes a large share of the gap (>30%), feasible now, a one-step causal chain inside the scope, and the market in the brief is not moving against it.\n51  - 🟡 **Medium** — closes 10–30%, needs coordination, still direct.\n52  - 🟢 **Long-term** — closes <10%, or carries a cross-functional dependency. Rare: most actions are 🔴/🟡.\n53  \n54  An action whose chain is multi-step, or whose scope is partly another segment's, is 🟡 at most — the one-sentence test fails for it.\n55  \n56  ## Rules\n57  \n58  1. **Scope-locked** — the analysis chose the segment, product and geography; an action outside it answers a question nobody asked.\n59  2. **Ground in data** — every action names the metric and gap from the analysis. A figure in the Why is an actual from evidence or a tagged figure from the brief, and the reader can tell which.\n60  3. **The brief is the context** — it is the wiki and market pass for this question; a second grep finds the same page with less of its context, and a wiki path or URL in the answer is one the brief cites.\n61  4. **Be concrete** — \"Increase CASA\" gives the owner nothing to start on; \"CASA payroll for HH customers earning ≥30M, target +20K B in Q3\" does.\n62  5. **Max 5 actions** — ordered by priority; a longer list buries the ones that matter.\n63  6. **Match the user's language** — Vietnamese question → Vietnamese actions; English → English.\n64  7. **No borrowed campaigns** — a campaign aimed at another segment stays out, even \"adapted\": its `[campaign]` line in the brief carries the scope it was designed for.\n65  8. **Cross-segment insights** go in a separate \"Additional Notes\", not in the action list — the reader owns one segment.\n66  9. **No TCB plan found is still a gap** — propose from banking expertise and say the plan was not found; a gap left without an action reads as an oversight, and a proposed lever passed off as an existing one is worse.\n67  \n68  ## HTML report integration\n69  \n70  Placement: `SECTIONS → … → ACTIONS → FOOTER`. Uses priority badges (🔴/🟡/🟢) · card layout (Why/Action/Impact/Owner) · collapsible details.","duration_ms":295,"run_ms":4,"gen_ms":265}}

Loan rate = Base Rate + Spread (biên độ). Base rates defined for floating (monthly reset) and fixed modes in VND/USD. Spread determined by credit rating (× 12 grades Aaa–D) and collateral coverage ratio (× 6 bands from ≥0-10% to ≥100%). Preferential rate reductions (§5 PL05) allow stacking up to 8 incentive types, floored at base rate.\n 13  \n 14  ## Detail\n 15  \n 16  ### 1. Base Rate Structure (Lãi suất cơ sở)\n 17  \n 18  Base rates are set by Treasury and communicated periodically. Two modes:\n 19  \n 20  #### 1.1 Floating Base Rate (Thả nổi — monthly reset)\n 21  \n 22  Applied to loans with periodic rate adjustment (1 month cycle).\n 23  \n 24  | Key | Name | Applicable To | Rate |\n 25  |---|---|---|---|\n 26  | LSCS VND Short-term | Lãi suất cơ sở VND ngắn hạn | SME/MSME VND ST loans | ~4.67%–10.0% |\n 27  | LSCS VND Medium/Long-term | Lãi suất cơ sở VND trung dài hạn | SME/MSME VND MLT loans | (set periodically) |\n 28  | LSCS USD | Lãi suất cơ sở USD | SME/MSME USD loans | ~4.25%–4.67% |\n 29  \n 30  #### 1.2 Fixed Base Rate (Cố định)\n 31  \n 32  Applied to fixed-rate loans.\n 33  \n 34  **VND loans:**\n 35  \n 36  | Tenor | 1M–3M | 4M–6M | 7M–12M |\n 37  |---|---|---|---|\n 38  | Rate | 8.2% | 8.5% | 8.9% |\n 39  \n 40  **USD loans:**\n 41  \n 42  | Tenor | 1M | 2M | 3M | 4M | 5M | 6M | 7M–12M |\n 43  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n 44  | Rate | 4.67% | (declining) | ... | ... | ... | ... | 4.25% |\n 45  \n 46  **VND loans at FCY rate (sản phẩm 110305, 110406, chiết khấu 318):**\n 47  \n 48  | Product | 1M | 3M | 6M | 12M |\n 49  |---|---|---|---|---|\n 50  | Chiết khấu 318 | 5.10% | (declining) | ... | 4.68% |\n 51  | SP 110305 | 5.10% | ... | ... | 4.68% |\n 52  | SP 110406 | 5.10% | ... | ... | 4.68% |\n 53  \n 54  ### 2. Spread Framework (Biên độ lãi suất)\n 55  \n 56  #### 2.1 Group I Spread (Biên độ nhóm I)\n 57  \n 58  For loans secured 100% by Group I collateral: **Flat 1.0%**\n 59  \n 60  #### 2.2 Group II Spread — Short-term Standard\n 61  \n 62  Matrix of spread by **credit rating** × **collateral/limit ratio**:\n 63  \n 64  | Rating | ≥100% | ≥80-100% | ≥50-80% | ≥30-50% | ≥10-30% | 0-10% |\n 65  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n 66  | Aaa-Aa2 | 0.71% | 0.82% | 0.97% | 1.08% | 1.25% | 1.76% |\n 67  | Aa3-A1 | 0.93% | 1.08% | 1.26% | 1.41% | 1.64% | 2.31% |\n 68  | A2 | 1.22% | 1.41% | 1.66% | 1.85% | 2.15% | 3.02% |\n 69  | A3 | 1.60% | 1.85% | 2.17% | 2.43% | 2.82% | 3.97% |\n 70  | Baa1-Baa2 | 2.10% | 2.43% | 2.85% | 3.19% | 3.70% | 5.20% |\n 71  | Baa3 | 2.75% | 3.19% | 3.74% | 4.18% | 4.85% | 6.83% |\n 72  | Ba1 | 2.86% | 3.31% | 3.88% | 4.34% | 5.04% | 7.09% |\n 73  | Ba2 | 2.97% | 3.44% | 4.03% | 4.51% | 5.23% | 7.37% |\n 74  | Ba3 | 3.08% | 3.57% | 4.19% | 4.68% | 5.43% | 7.65% |\n 75  | B1 | 3.20% | 3.71% | 4.35% | 4.87% | 5.65% | 7.95% |\n 76  | B2-B3 | 3.33% | 3.86% | 4.52% | 5.06% | 5.87% | 8.26% |\n 77  | D | 6.85% | 7.94% | 9.30% | 10.41% | 12.08% | 24.16% |\n 78  \n 79  **Key rules:**\n 80  - MISA channel loans: Standard spread + 1.0%\n 81  - Overdraft (thấu chi): Standard spread + 0.5%\n 82  - Collateral ratio = Total Group II collateral / Total lending limit (not per-loan)\n 83  \n 84  #### 2.3 Medium/Long-term Spread (TSCD investment)\n 85  \n 86  **Fixed-rate initial period:**\n 87  \n 88  | Period | Fully Secured | Under-secured | Min Tenor |\n 89  |---|---|---|---|\n 90  | 6M fixed | 10% | 13% | 12M |\n 91  | 12M fixed | 11% | 14% | 18M |\n 92  \n 93  **Floating spread (subsequent period):**\n 94  \n 95  | Rating | Fully Secured | Under-secured |\n 96  |---|---|---|\n 97  | Aaa-Baa2 | 2.80% | 5.93% |\n 98  | Baa3-Ba3 | 3.00% | 6.79% |\n 99  | B1-B3 | 4.00% | 8.40% |\n100  | D | 5.00% | 10.91% |\n101  \n102  ### 3. Collateral Classification (48 Codes)\n103  \n104  Group II collateral (“Tài sản cơ bản”) includes 48 asset types:\n105  - Real estate: residential, land, apartment, hotel, agricultural land, project development rights\n106  - Vehicles: PTVT (transportation)\n107  - Financial: certificates of deposit, savings, bonds, treasury bills\n108  - Other: investment projects, development rights\n109  \n110  Collateral value = Maximum lending value per asset code.\n111  \n112  ### 4. Preferential Rate Reductions (PL05)\n113  \n114  Incentives are **stackable** (cộng dồn) subject to floor = base rate for each tenor. Apply to **short-term loans only**, not combinable with other promotional programs or exception approvals.\n115  \n116  | # | Incentive | Reduction | Conditions |\n117  |---|---|---|---|\n118  | 1 | Bundle Banca (BANCA) | 0.5% (if ≥1% coverage) or 1.0% (if ≥2%) | Min 10M VND bond value. Valid to 31/12/2026 |\n119  | 2 | Payroll 2-way ≥10 staff (Biz_Payroll) | 0.2% | Review every 3 months. Valid 01/04–30/09/2026 |\n120  | 3 | Business Owner = Priority/Private (VV1PFS0615) | 1.0–1.5% (floor: LSCS+0.5%) | Truly Priority: -1.5%; Not Truly: -1.0% |\n121  | 4 | BO transfers mortgage to TCB (BO_MG) | 0.2% | Min 10% of SME limit. Until limit expiry |\n122  | 5 | Refinancing (Biz_TTT) | 0.5% | Min 2B VND for MLT transfers |\n123  | 6 | Refinancing 2 — HO list (SME_TTT_MG) | 1.5% (floor: LSCS+0.5%) | 2 months from effective date |\n124  | 7 | AUM ratio (TD+CD+CASA+AE) | 0.1%–0.4% | 30-50%: -0.1%; 50-100%: -0.2%; 100-150%: -0.3%; >150%: -0.4% |\n125  | 8 | Imex profile (VV1PFS1013) | Max 1.5% (floor: LSCS+0.5%) | Must use ≥4 products, CASA/lending ≥15%, EOP STL +0.5B. Valid to 30/09/2026 |\n126  \n127  **Approval**: Branch Director / Regional Director via Pricing Tool. Max 3-month validity per approval.\n128  \n129  ### 5. Rate Calculation Formula\n130  \n131  ```\n132  Loan Rate = Base Rate + Spread - Preferential Reduction\n133  \n134  Where:\n135  - Base Rate: Per §1 (floating or fixed, by tenor & currency)\n136  - Spread: Per §2 (rating × collateral matrix)\n137  - Preferential: Per §4 (stackable, floored at Base Rate)\n138  - Floor: Loan Rate ≥ Base Rate (for preferential cases)\n139  ```\n140  \n\n[Read 140 lines (lines 1-140 of 153 total). 13 lines remaining from offset 140.]","duration_ms":99,"run_ms":99}}

Customer increase follows TCB wallet share increase\n127  - Ticket size growth aligned with industry benchmarks\n128  - Lower NIM (3.6%) reflecting secured nature and larger ticket sizes\n129  - RoRWA improvement driven by OC efficiency (scale) rather than NIM expansion\n130  \n131  ### Actions to Boost RoRWA\n132  \n133  1. **Expand customer book** by accepting lower-rated customers while controlling risk through increasing transaction value and lending rates\n134  2. **Improve recovery rate** by 15% per year through strengthening debt collection capability\n135  3. **Reduce RWA** by 15% through regular updates of customer financial reports (SBV compliance)\n136  \n137  ### Funnel Conversion Analysis\n138  \n139  Current funnel from 110K holding customers to ~5,000 with credit (5% penetration):\n140  \n141  | Stage | Exclusion Rate | Reason |\n142  |-------|---------------|--------|\n143  | Business rule filter | 20% | Non-targeted biz type (FDI, SOEs, NGOs, legal offices) |\n144  | | 80% of above | Hit establishment duration threshold |\n145  | Fraud rules | 50% | Improper use of funds & cashflow generation |\n146  | | 3% | Blacklist/watchlist |\n147  | | 7% | LR/CA information duplication |\n148  | Credit rules | 13% | Credit history of customer & LR |\n149  | | 7% | Rating cut-off |\n150  | | 10% | Duration of relationship with TCB |\n151  | Demand gap | 30% | Approved but no expressed credit demand |\n152  | | 14% | No lending demand at all |\n153  | RM coverage | 85% | Approved but NOT visited on-site by RM |\n154  \n155  Target: 15%+ credit penetration by 2030 (2-3x from current).\n156  \n157  ## Open Questions / Caveats\n158  - Financial projections are management estimates from Bluebook (July 2025), subject to annual plan updates\n159  - RoRWA methodology assumed to follow [[rorwa-methodology]] — verify if SME-specific adjustments apply\n160  - Fully unsecured NIM of 7.8% is aspirational and depends on rating mix evolution\n161  - Recovery rate improvement of 15%/yr needs validation against collection infrastructure plans\n162  - Funnel conversion targets (5% → 15%) require coordinated effort across Risk, IT, DA, and frontline\n163  \n164  ## See Also\n165  - data: {"type":"response.output_item.done","custom_outputs":null,"item":{"type":"function_call_output","call_id":"sa_9cf222ca","output":" 1  # RoRWA Methodology\n 2  \n 3  ## Metadata\n 4  - Domain: bf-general\n 5  - Category: methodology\n 6  - Tags: rorwa, return-on-rwa, profitability, risk-adjusted-return, EL\n 7  - Last updated: 2026-05-15\n 8  - Source: D1_Business_Rules-Metrics__RoRWA__RoRWA Procedure update 2025.md, D1_Business_Rules-Metrics__RoRWA__Truyền thông RoRWA - Tính toán theo TT41_BD (2).md\n 9  \n10  ## Summary\n11  \n12  RoRWA (Return on Risk-Weighted Assets) is the primary profitability metric used across Techcombank to evaluate customer-level and portfolio-level performance on a risk-adjusted basis. The formula is: **RoRWA = (TOI - RC - OC) / RWA**. It incorporates total operating income, risk costs (Expected Loss), operating costs, and regulatory capital consumption.\n13  \n14  ## Detail\n15  \n16  ### Formula\n17  \n18  **RoRWA = (TOI - RC - OC) / RWA**\n19  \n20  From 2019, RoRWA is calculated as a trailing 12-month figure for each customer.\n21  \n22  ### Component 1: TOI (Total Operating Income)\n23  \n24  **Formula:** TOI = NII Loan + NII Bond + NII TD + NII CASA + NFI + NFX + Other Income\n25  \n26  TOI represents the total revenue generated from a customer across all product relationships.\n27  \n28  **Sub-components:**\n29  - **TOI of credit products**: NII Lending (margin × avg bal), TOI LC/BG (margin × avg bal), Funding NII from CASA/TD/CD (avg bal × margin)\n30  - **TOI cross-sell**: FX income (val & margin), other fee products, other TOI\n31  \n32  **Notes:**\n33  - TOI danh mục: Excludes TOI from FI (Financial Institution) customers\n34  - TOI from subsidized lending programs approved by the Bank is excluded when calculating RoRWA at bloc level\n35  \n36  **Data source:** Management reports — FIN\n37  \n38  ### Component 2: RC (Risk Cost)\n39  \n40  **Formula:** RC = EL(%) × Avg(Loan + Bond)\n41  \n42  RC represents the expected credit loss, calculated using the [[expected-loss-model]].\n43  \n44  **Notes:**\n45  - For each customer: EL(%) is determined from monthly QTRR (risk management) reports\n46  - EL is calculated based on end-of-month Exposure figures\n47  - EAD amount from risk may differ from CAR engine data (more conservative)\n48  \n49  **Data source:** EL Model — RMD; Management reports — FIN\n50  \n\n[Read 50 lines (lines 1-50 of 192 total). 142 lines remaining from offset 50.]","duration_ms":78,"run_ms":78}}
